# C0086 | unique-exact-body-sender-within-2s | [{"source":"new","record":77,"line":121,"at":"2026-08-31T17:40:21","archive":"2026-10-19"},{"source":"old","record":75,"line":119,"at":"2026-08-31T17:40:21","archive":"2026-10-19"},{"archive":"2026-10-23","source":"continued-2026-10-23","record":77,"line":121,"at":"2026-08-31T17:40:21"}]
[2026-08-31T17:40:21] ~ Sujay H S: I was talking to a Palliative Medicine Colleague. Just putting it here for the group to decide. She was suggesting a one stop shop for Palliative Care
- Clinical Case History
- Goals of care discussions and consent forms - Important
- Network of nearest palliative care centres 
- Network of patient support groups
- Network of home care centres, blood banks, or something where they can get stuff like oxygen from
- Network of Closest RMIs (Registered Medical Institutes) - Where they can dispense morphine - This may be important
- Some Medical Education Features for basic symptoms like breathlessness, cough, and pain and what not
- AI Chatbot to reduce anxiety and to decide when it may be good to visit the doctor rather than just dealing with it on their own
- Emergency phone numbers to contact in case of crisis

I understand that incorporating these may dilute what we want to do. Just put up the ideas here if anyone wants to opine on any of this

# C0108 | unique-exact-body-sender-within-2s | [{"source":"new","record":99,"line":178,"at":"2026-09-08T22:01:11","archive":"2026-10-19"},{"source":"old","record":97,"line":176,"at":"2026-09-08T22:01:11","archive":"2026-10-19"},{"archive":"2026-10-23","source":"continued-2026-10-23","record":99,"line":178,"at":"2026-09-08T22:01:11"}]
[2026-09-08T22:01:11] Shaik Awaiz: The Health-a-thon 2026 Connect video is an orientation and Q&A session designed to clarify the goals, structure, and guidelines of the upcoming hackathon
.
Here is a detailed, structured summary of the key details from the session:
1. Core Collaborative Ecosystem & Vision
The Organizations Behind It: The event is an initiative by the Koita Foundation and the Koita Center for Digital Health (KCDH) at IIT Bombay, in collaboration with Ashoka University's Center for Digital Health, the National Cancer Grid (NCG), RSSDI, and FOGSI, powered by Reskilll
.
Bridging the Silos: A primary motivation for the hackathon is that in India, domains like banking and travel have rapidly digitized, while healthcare has lagged because engineers and medical professionals specialize early and work in silos
.
Strong Community Response: The hackathon has already garnered 2,000+ registrations, including 500+ doctors and medical students, alongside 1,500+ technologists and engineering students
.
Host Organizations:
Koita Foundation: An 8-year-old not-for-profit focused entirely on the adoption of digital health and AI in India, founded by Mr. Rizwan Koita (founder of CitiusTech, India's 25th unicorn)
. Rizwan Koita also chairs NABH, which defines digital health standards in India
.
National Cancer Grid (NCG): A network of 350+ cancer hospitals managing ~65% of India's cancer load
.
RSSDI: Asia's largest body of diabetologists, representing roughly 13,000 doctors
.
FOGSI: A Center of Excellence in obstetrics and gynaecology representing 45,000+ clinicians
.
2. Core Tenets & Unique Rules
The organizers emphasize that two key features make this hackathon completely unique:
A. Mandatory Doctor-Technologist Duo
Team Size: Teams must consist of 2 to 5 members
.
Mandatory Structure: Every single team must have at least one qualified doctor (clinical lead) and at least one technology professional
.
Who Qualifies as a Clinical Lead? MBBS qualified doctors (and above), PG medical students/doctors, and BAMS, BHMS, BUMS, or BDS qualified doctors
. Additionally, 4th-year MBBS students and above in their rotating internship can act as clinical leads
.
Frontline Specialists: Frontline coordinators (like chemotherapy coordinators, clinical pharmacists, or nurses) are encouraged to join, but they cannot replace the doctor requirement; the team must still include a qualified clinician to remain eligible
.
B. Strictly Non-Clinical Use Cases
The Reason: Clinical AI solutions (like diagnostic risk predictors, X-ray/MRI imaging analysis, or dosage calculations) face massive regulatory uncertainties, data-access hurdles, and scaling challenges in India
.
The Focus: This hackathon is exclusively focused on non-clinical, administrative, operational, financial, or assistive use cases
. These solutions do not require regulatory clearances and can be piloted and scaled immediately
.
Where the Line is Drawn:
Clinical (Out of Scope): Any app that computes a drug's dosage, interprets scans, or guides a doctor/patient on clinical care pathways
.
Non-Clinical (In Scope): Any application assisting with workflows, operations, reminders, or billing
. For example, a medication reminder app is non-clinical, whereas an app telling a patient what medicine to take based on symptoms is clinical
.
Illustrative Examples of Non-Clinical AI Use Cases:
Hospital Operations: Simplifying patient registration, accelerating patient discharge processes, room booking, staff scheduling, or backend inventory management
.
Finance & Claims: Analyzing billing data to coordinate insurance claim settlements (TPA tracking)
.
Patient Engagement & Care: Automated medication reminders, patient education, caregiver tools, and neonatal care tracking
.
Agentic AI Solutions: Utilizing autonomous AI agents (leveraging modern LLM systems) for chronic clinic follow-ups and patient care coordination
.
3. Three Focused Problem Tracks
Teams must align their ideas with at least one of three longitudinal health tracks:
Cancer Care (focusing on long-term follow-ups and patient engagement)
.
Diabetes (chronic management over years or decades)
.
Maternal Health (Mother and Child) (neonatal cases, postpartum support, and tools for caregivers/mothers)
.
Note: If an idea is general-purpose (applies broadly to clinic operations), the team must still apply under one specific track for prize and shortlisting evaluation
.
4. Roadmap, Timeline, & Evaluation
Phase 1: Registration & Team Formation (Current): Teams can register and complete their compositions on the dashboard
. Registration closes around 25th September
.
Phase 2: Idea Submission (12th September – 25th September): Teams submit their target use case, proposed solution, technology approach, and build plans
.
Note: A working prototype is not required at this stage; supporting materials like PPTs or mock-up videos are welcome
.
Evaluation Lenses: Ideas are evaluated based on Healthcare Relevance & Workflow Fit (impact and practicality) and Technical Feasibility & AI Engineering
.
Phase 3: Build Sprint (4–5 Weeks): Shortlisted teams build their prototypes
. Each team is assigned two structured mentor check-ins to receive feedback, refine their prototype, and practice their pitch
.
Grand Finale (28th November, 2026): The top 30 teams (10 per track) will be invited to IIT Bombay for an offline presentation and live demo
.
5. Key Q&A & Participant Insights
Matchmaking & Team Formation: Technologists without a doctor, and clinicians without a developer, can use the platform's matchmaking tool (which suggests pairings based on shared interest tracks) or join the active WhatsApp community to form teams
.
Dataset Availability: The organizers will not provide datasets
. Teams must utilize open-source platforms (such as the Government of India's AI coach) or collaborate with their team's doctor to leverage real-world clinical practice datasets
.
Modifying Submissions: Teams can edit their chosen problem statements or reshuffle team members directly on the dashboard via the "edit use case and problem" button
.
Use of Pre-existing Software: Teams cannot present pre-existing, live commercial products as-is
. However, they can build innovative features or novel workflows on top of existing software components (such as integrating an administrative scheduling layer onto a standard voice-scribe application)
.
Hardware Solutions: Hardware-related projects are permitted if the team already has the hardware to demonstrate, though app-based solutions are generally easier to build in the hackathon's timeframe
.
Incentives & Career Upside:
Cash prizes will be awarded to the top 10 teams
.
Finalists at IIT Bombay will pitch directly to India’s leading national clinical figures (including the president of FOGSI, RSSDI leaders, and the NCG national convenor) and members of the venture capital community
.
Rizwan Koita highlights that a previous winner of a Koita Foundation award (Adalat AI) went on to receive funding from Y Combinator, proving the incredible visibility and scaling opportunities this hackathon offers
.

# C0289 | unique-exact-body-sender-within-2s | [{"source":"new","record":262,"line":482,"at":"2026-09-24T09:48:54","archive":"2026-10-19"},{"source":"old","record":260,"line":480,"at":"2026-09-24T09:48:54","archive":"2026-10-19"},{"archive":"2026-10-23","source":"continued-2026-10-23","record":262,"line":482,"at":"2026-09-24T09:48:54"}]
[2026-09-24T09:48:54] ~ Sujay H S: Kalyan — honest answer: *a detailed scoring rubric has not been published*, and I am not going to invent one and have you optimise against something I made up.

What I can give you is better than a guess: *the form's own questions are the criteria.* Nothing is scored that you were not asked. So the entry that answers each question well is the entry that scores well.

*What Round 1 actually asks you for:*
• Which track you are submitting under
• Who the primary user is — clinician, patient, or other
• The use case or your own problem statement
• *The problem* — what is broken, in the real world
• *The solution* — what you will build
• Your *tech stack*
• Any *prior work* on this
• Your *pitch deck* — the only required attachment
• A confirmation that your solution stays *assistive*: it does not diagnose, recommend treatment, provide clinical decision support, or give autonomous clinical advice

*On your specific examples:*
• *Alignment with a problem statement* — yes, this matters. Make your chosen track and your problem description agree with each other.
• *Effective use of AI* — there is no requirement to use AI at all. Use it if the problem needs it. A simple solution to a real problem beats AI applied for its own sake.
• *Demo video of a prototype* — *optional.* Prototype link, video and supporting document are all optional. Only the pitch deck is required. Do not delay submitting to make a video.

*The one thing I would say carries the most weight:* that out-of-scope confirmation is not a formality. A solution that diagnoses or prescribes is outside what this programme is for, whatever else it does well.

If a formal rubric is published before tomorrow I will post it here immediately.

*Round 1 closes tomorrow, Friday 25 September, 11:59 PM IST.*
